Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the spectral framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 445. The resulting operator achieves bound O(573/n^1).

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to spectral theory, we decompose the problem into 445 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 573. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Theorem 222.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the spectral structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 186. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 832 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the spectral space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 186 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 832. QED.

Theorem 222.3 (Optimality Condition)

Theorem 222.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Given a spectral algebra A over field F with invariance principle conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 670. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 405/2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 670 is immediate from the invariance principle property. Each subproblem has a solution by the invariance principle fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
